<<<PROMPT_HISTORY_CONTENT 1 LENGTH 2411>>>
SUMMARY
The wooden floor creaks as Amy wipes a polished table with a cloth soaked in ale-scented oil. She wears a rough-spun tunic and leather apron that show the scuffs of her medieval trade. A goblin with green skin leans over the counter, its tiny hands clutching a chipped mug; Amy refills it from a barrel beside the hearth.

A beautiful female elf steps in, silver hair streaming down its shoulders, and takes a seat at the back table. Amy pours a steaming cup into a crystal chalice for the elf, setting it gently on the wood with a nod of thanks.

From the doorway, a dragon-shaped creature slides onto a high bar stool, scales glittering like polished obsidian. Its wings unfurl to reveal a pair of amber eyes that scan the room. Amy greets the newcomer with a warm smile and lifts a crystal cup from her shelf, pouring a special brew into it before handing it over.

The dragon licks its lips, then takes a slow sip, letting the liquid swirl across its tongue. Amy watches as the creature exhales contentedly; she wipes the bar with a fresh cloth, smoothing out any drips of ale that have fallen onto the wood.

Finally, Amy turns off the lanterns at each table and pulls a wooden latch on the back door. She steps outside to close it, locking the tavern for the night as the moon rises over the medieval town.

The entire story must be able to take place within a 48-second timeframe.

Preserve every explicit event and outcome in the summary in the same order.
Every explicit event in the summary must happen visibly in the story. Do not
compress, imply, or skip an explicit transition. If a character moves from one
location to another, write the movement clearly enough that the character's
location is unambiguous. Describe physical actions literally: make attachment,
movement, action targets, containers, and destinations explicit. Avoid poetic or
figurative wording when it could change how an action or object is visualized.
Write enough concrete sequential action to support 6 distinct film beats.
You may add connective staging and concrete detail, but do not add a new major
plot event, outcome, or named character.
The source's final explicit event must also be the expanded story's final event.
Do not continue past it with a new destination, containment, escape, surviving
threat, resolution, or aftermath.
Write one continuous story, not an outline, beat list, screenplay, or analysis.

SUMMARY
The wooden floor creaks as Amy wipes a polished table with a cloth soaked in ale-scented oil. She wears a rough-spun tunic and leather apron that show the scuffs of her medieval trade. A goblin with green skin leans over the counter, its tiny hands clutching a chipped mug; Amy refills it from a barrel beside the hearth.

A beautiful female elf steps in, silver hair streaming down its shoulders, and takes a seat at the back table. Amy pours a steaming cup into a crystal chalice for the elf, setting it gently on the wood with a nod of thanks.

From the doorway, a dragon-shaped creature slides onto a high bar stool, scales glittering like polished obsidian. Its wings unfurl to reveal a pair of amber eyes that scan the room. Amy greets the newcomer with a warm smile and lifts a crystal cup from her shelf, pouring a special brew into it before handing it over.

The dragon licks its lips, then takes a slow sip, letting the liquid swirl across its tongue. Amy watches as the creature exhales contentedly; she wipes the bar with a fresh cloth, smoothing out any drips of ale that have fallen onto the wood.

Finally, Amy turns off the lanterns at each table and pulls a wooden latch on the back door. She steps outside to close it, locking the tavern for the night as the moon rises over the medieval town.

STORY
The wooden floor creaks as Amy wipes a polished table with a cloth soaked in ale‑scented oil. She wears her rough‑spun tunic and leather apron that show scuffs from her medieval trade. A goblin with green skin leans over the counter, its tiny hands clutching a chipped mug; Amy refills it from a barrel beside the hearth.

An elf with silver hair streaming down its shoulders steps in and takes a seat at the back table. Amy pours a steaming cup into a crystal chalice for the elf, setting the chalice gently on the wood while nodding thanks.

From the doorway, a dragon‑shaped creature slides onto a high bar stool; scales glitter like polished obsidian. Its wings unfurl to reveal amber eyes that scan the room. Amy greets the newcomer with a warm smile and lifts a crystal cup from her shelf, pouring a special brew into it before handing it over.

The dragon licks its lips, then takes a slow sip, letting liquid swirl across its tongue. Amy watches as the creature exhales contentedly; she wipes the bar with a fresh cloth, smoothing out any drips of ale that have fallen onto the wood.

Finally, Amy turns off the lanterns at each table and pulls a wooden latch on the back door. She steps outside to close it, locking the tavern for the night as the moon rises over the medieval town.

Convert the complete story above into exactly 6 sequential beats.
Preserve the story's event order and outcomes. Do not summarize away an action
that must visibly happen on screen. Preserve explicit story lists of distinct
participants, recipients, targets, or objects instead of collapsing them into a
generic group label when the listed identities matter to the visible event.
Each beat must be executable as one film clip.
A terminal result belongs to one beat only: if the next beat begins with that
result or consequence, the current beat must stop before it.
Do not create functional identity labels for unnamed participants. Describe the
story event naturally; Subject identity is resolved later from the staged RAW scene.


RETURN
{"beats":[{"beat_number":1,"beat_text":"one sentence"}]}

ACCEPTED BEAT
The wooden floor creaks as Amy wipes a polished table with a cloth soaked in ale‑scented oil, her rough‑spun tunic and leather apron scuffed from medieval trade.

Return only facts that are true at the END of ACCEPTED BEAT and that should
remain available to later beats. Capture persistent world facts broadly; Python
will decide later which facts are relevant enough to inject into prompts.

Important rules:
- ACCEPTED BEAT is already semantically valid. Do not judge or repair it.
- When the beat concretizes an abstract incoming fact, preserve the concrete
  realization. Example: incoming location "safe location" plus "Will enters the
  back closet" means Will.location = "back closet".
- Record explicit final character locations, containment, held/equipped/stored
  objects, injuries, conditions, and other persistent character facts.
- Record explicit persistent environment facts. New rooms/areas may go under
  environment.rooms, and concrete objects may go under environment.objects.
  It is acceptable to remember an incidental persistent object such as a blue
  vase; capture is broader than prompt injection.
- Record barriers/doors/windows and threat state when the beat explicitly
  establishes their persistent final state.
- Use threats only for hostile or dangerous entities. Ordinary people, victims,
  or protected characters belong under characters, not threats.
- Use existing character/entity names from CURRENT CANONICAL STATE when the beat
  clearly refers to them.
- If ACCEPTED BEAT explicitly introduces a distinct threat with wording such as
  "another", "new", "second", or "third", emit a separate threat entry instead
  of updating an existing threat of the same type. Python will assign its stable
  canonical threat ID.
- Do not merge distinct entities merely because their types are similar.
- Do not output transient pose, camera, lighting, momentary action, dialogue,
  sound, emotion, or speculation.
- Do not output story_progress. Python owns required-event bookkeeping.
- Output only changed or newly established facts. Do not copy unchanged defaults.
- Top-level state_patch keys may only be: characters, environment, threats, story.
- These four roots are siblings. Never nest characters, environment, threats, or story inside one another.
- If no persistent fact is established, return an empty state_patch object.

Important rules:
- ACCEPTED BEAT is already semantically valid. Do not judge or repair it.
- When the beat concretizes an abstract incoming fact, preserve the concrete
  realization. Example: incoming location "safe location" plus "Will enters the
  back closet" means Will.location = "back closet".

  clearly refers to them.

  "another", "new", "second", or "third", emit a separate threat entry instead
  of updating an existing threat of the same type. Python will assign its stable
  canonical threat ID.
- Do not merge distinct entities merely because their types are similar.
- Do not output transient pose, camera, lighting, momentary action, dialogue,
  sound, emotion, or speculation.
- Do not output story_progress. Python owns required-event bookkeeping.
- Output only changed or newly established facts. Do not copy unchanged defaults.
- Top-level state_patch keys may only be: characters, environment, threats, story.
- These four roots are siblings. Never nest characters, environment, threats, or story inside one another.

moving someone else there, standing near it, or merely beginning toward it does
not satisfy the effect. If CURRENT JOB says the actor returns to a location, the
candidate must actually show that return before ending. Possession is not equipping;
injury is not death; picked up then set down is not held at the end. For entities
already in CURRENT STATE or STATE EFFECTS IF VALID, new persistent changes require
a matching assigned effect. Do not require effects for new incidental entities or
temporary motion that restores the prior state. Boundary, barrier, door/window,
and containment changes are intentionally outside this validation phase.

{"valid": false, "issue": "short concrete explanation"}. No extra keys or markdown.

The wooden floor creaks as Amy wipes a polished table with a cloth soaked in ale‑scented oil. She wears her rough‑spun tunic and leather apron that show scuffs from her medieval trade. A goblin with green skin leans over the counter, its tiny hands clutching a chipped mug; Amy refills it from a barrel beside the hearth.

An elf with silver hair streaming down its shoulders steps in and takes a seat at the back table. Amy pours a steaming cup into a crystal chalice for the elf, setting the chalice gently on the wood while nodding thanks.

From the doorway, a dragon‑shaped creature slides onto a high bar stool; scales glitter like polished obsidian. Its wings unfurl to reveal amber eyes that scan the room. Amy greets the newcomer with a warm smile and lifts a crystal cup from her shelf, pouring a special brew into it before handing it over.

The dragon licks its lips, then takes a slow sip, letting liquid swirl across its tongue. Amy watches as the creature exhales contentedly; she wipes the bar with a fresh cloth, smoothing out any drips of ale that have fallen onto the wood.

Finally, Amy turns off the lanterns at each table and pulls a wooden latch on the back door. She steps outside to close it, locking the tavern for the night as the moon rises over the medieval town.

  canonical threat ID.

  sound, emotion, or speculation.

EXPANDED STORY
The wooden floor creaks as Amy wipes a polished table with a cloth soaked in ale‑scented oil. She wears her rough‑spun tunic and leather apron that show scuffs from her medieval trade. A goblin with green skin leans over the counter, its tiny hands clutching a chipped mug; Amy refills it from a barrel beside the hearth.

An elf with silver hair streaming down its shoulders steps in and takes a seat at the back table. Amy pours a steaming cup into a crystal chalice for the elf, setting the chalice gently on the wood while nodding thanks.

From the doorway, a dragon‑shaped creature slides onto a high bar stool; scales glitter like polished obsidian. Its wings unfurl to reveal amber eyes that scan the room. Amy greets the newcomer with a warm smile and lifts a crystal cup from her shelf, pouring a special brew into it before handing it over.

The dragon licks its lips, then takes a slow sip, letting liquid swirl across its tongue. Amy watches as the creature exhales contentedly; she wipes the bar with a fresh cloth, smoothing out any drips of ale that have fallen onto the wood.

SPATIAL REFINEMENT
The tavern occupies a rectangular interior space measuring **thirty feet east‑west by forty feet north‑south** (about 1200 sq ft).  
The room’s layout is anchored by the back door and the stone hearth.

**Back Door (West wall, center)** – A wooden latch attaches to a 3‑foot wide frame that sits one foot above floor level. The doorway opens eastward into the tavern and provides direct access from the street to the back of the room.

**Stone Hearth (South wall, centered)** – A rectangular hearth runs twelve feet along the south wall with an eight‑foot depth into the space. Its fire surface measures six by four feet and is surrounded on three sides by a stone surround that rises two feet above floor level.

**Barrel (East side of hearth)** – An oak barrel lies against the southern wall, immediately east of the hearth’s center. The barrel has a diameter of two feet and stands five feet tall, filling the space just outside the hearth’s fire surface.

**Counter (North wall, centered between door and entrance)** – A polished counter stretches twelve feet along the north wall with a width of three feet projecting into the tavern. It is positioned so that its front edge lies one foot from the northern wall, leaving ample room for movement.

**Polished Tables (Central area)** – Six rectangular tables are set across the middle of the space: each table measures four by six feet and sits on the creaking wooden floor. They are spaced at least three feet apart to allow easy passage between them and toward the counter or hearth.

**Back Table (West side, near back door)** – A single larger table is placed behind the bar stool, two feet east of the back door’s center. It measures five by seven feet and sits on a raised plinth that keeps it one foot above floor level for easy seating.

**High Bar Stool (North wall, front of counter)** – One high bar stool stands at the north‑west corner of the counter. The stool is 3 ft tall, with a seat width of 1½ ft and depth of 1 ft, positioned so patrons can sit opposite the entrance.

**Crystal Shelf (East wall, between hearth and counter)** – A crystal shelf runs eight feet along the east wall, rising four feet above floor level. It projects one foot into the room and holds polished crystal glasses.

**Lanterns (At each table)** – Each of the six tables has a small lantern on its surface: a 2‑foot tall stand holding an oil lamp that illuminates the table’s immediate area without blocking movement.

The wooden floor consists of four‑inch planks spaced half an inch apart, giving it a characteristic creak when walked upon. All objects are arranged so no two overlap; each door and passageway remains clear for easy access throughout the tavern.

The tavern is a rectangular room thirty feet east‑west by forty feet north‑south, with a creaking wooden floor of four‑inch planks spaced half an inch apart. The west wall hosts the back door at its center; a wooden latch attaches to a three‑foot wide frame one foot above the floor and opens eastward into the tavern. The south wall contains a stone hearth centered along the wall, twelve feet long with an eight‑foot depth. Its fire surface is six by four feet, surrounded on three sides by a two‑foot high stone surround. East of the hearth’s center lies an oak barrel five feet tall and two feet in diameter against the southern wall. The east wall between the hearth and the north wall runs a crystal shelf eight feet long, rising four feet above floor level and projecting one foot into the room; it holds polished crystal glasses. The north wall hosts a polished counter twelve feet along its length with a three‑foot width projecting into the tavern. Its front edge sits one foot from the northern wall. At the north‑west corner of this counter stands a high bar stool, three feet tall with a seat 1½ by 1 foot in size. West of the back door, two feet east of its center, is a raised back table five by seven feet on a plinth keeping it one foot above floor level. The polished tables—six rectangular pieces each four by six feet—are set across the central area, spaced at least three feet apart to allow passage between them and toward the counter or hearth. Each of the six tables has a lantern stand two feet tall positioned on its surface; the lanterns hold oil lamps that illuminate their immediate areas without blocking movement. The entire space is anchored by the back door and stone hearth, with all other objects fixed in place relative to these anchors.

The north wall hosts a polished counter twelve feet along its length with a three‑foot width projecting into the tavern. Its front edge sits one foot from the northern wall. At the north‑west corner of this counter stands a high bar stool, three feet tall with a seat 1½ by 1 foot in size. West of the back door, two feet east of its center, is a raised back table five by seven feet on a plinth keeping it one foot above floor level. The polished tables—six rectangular pieces each four by six feet—are set across the central area, spaced at least three feet apart to allow passage between them and toward the counter or hearth. Each of the six tables has a lantern stand two feet tall positioned on its surface; the lanterns hold oil lamps that illuminate their immediate areas without blocking movement. The entire space is anchored by the back door and stone hearth, with all other objects fixed in place relative to these anchors.

RAW SCENE
Amy steps onto polished table 3, her sturdy brown leather boots brushing the wooden planks and producing a faint creak.  
She lifts a cloth soaked in ale‑scented oil from her apron pocket and presses it gently across the tabletop, wiping back and forth.  
The oil sheen glints off the wood as droplets of ale scent linger on the surface after each stroke.  
Her rough‑spun earth‑tone tunic shows visible scuffs along the sleeves and shoulders, while her dark brown leather apron bears worn patches from trade.

The west wall hosts the back door at its center; a wooden latch attaches to a three‑foot wide frame one foot above the floor and opens eastward into the tavern. The south wall contains a stone hearth centered along the wall, twelve feet long with an eight‑foot depth. Its fire surface is six by four feet, surrounded on three sides by a two‑foot high stone surround. East of the hearth’s center lies an oak barrel five feet tall and two feet in diameter against the southern wall. The east wall between the hearth and the north wall runs a crystal shelf eight feet long, rising four feet above floor level and projecting one foot into the room; it holds polished crystal glasses. The north wall hosts a polished counter twelve feet along its length with a three‑foot width projecting into the tavern. Its front edge sits one foot from the northern wall. At the north‑west corner of this counter stands a high bar stool, three feet tall with a seat 1½ by 1 foot in size. West of the back door, two feet east of its center, is a raised back table five by seven feet on a plinth keeping it one foot above floor level. The polished tables—six rectangular pieces each four by six feet—are set across the central area, spaced at least three feet apart to allow passage between them and toward the counter or hearth. Each of the six tables has a lantern stand two feet tall positioned on its surface; the lanterns hold oil lamps that illuminate their immediate areas without blocking movement. The entire space is anchored by the back door and stone hearth, with all other objects fixed in place relative to these anchors.

The goblin leans over the polished counter on the north wall, green skin glinting in the lantern light as he grips his chipped mug with both hands.

Amy steps back behind the counter, placing the spoon in her apron pocket, and stands beside the goblin with the freshly filled mug between them.

The north wall hosts a polished counter twelve feet along its length with a three‑foot width projecting into the tavern. Its front edge sits one foot from the northern wall. At the north‑west corner of this counter stands a high bar stool, three feet tall with a seat 1½ by 1 foot in size. West of the back door, two feet east of its center, is a raised back table five by seven feet on a plinth keeping it one foot above floor level. The polished tables—six rectangular pieces each four by six feet—are set across the central area, spaced at least three feet apart to allow passage between them and toward the counter or hearth. Each of the six tables has a lantern stand two feet tall positioned on its surface; the lanterns hold oil lamps that illuminate their immediate areas without blocking movement. The entire space is anchored by the back door and stone hearth, with all other objects fixed in place relative to these anchors.

RAW SCENE
The goblin leans over the polished counter on the north wall, green skin glinting in the lantern light as he grips his chipped mug with both hands.

Amy steps behind the counter, her earth‑tone tunic scuffed along the sleeves and shoulders, dark brown leather apron patched from trade. She walks toward the east side of the hearth where a wooden barrel rests against the southern wall, moving quickly—just enough to be seen in two quick shots: one as she strides across the creaking floor to the barrel, another as she returns.

She lifts a short wooden spoon from inside the barrel, scoops up frothy ale with it, and turns back toward the counter. With a swift motion she pours the ale into the goblin’s mug, filling it to the brim. The goblin leans forward slightly, eyes widening at the pour, his chipped mug now steaming.

Amy steps back behind the counter, placing the spoon in her apron pocket, and stands beside the goblin with the freshly filled mug between them.

STATIC SETTING AUTHORITY
The tavern is a rectangular room thirty feet east‑west by forty feet north‑south, with a creaking wooden floor of four‑inch planks spaced half an inch apart. The west wall hosts the back door at its center; a wooden latch attaches to a three‑foot wide frame one foot above the floor and opens eastward into the tavern. The south wall contains a stone hearth centered along the wall, twelve feet long with an eight‑foot depth. Its fire surface is six by four feet, surrounded on three sides by a two‑foot high stone surround. East of the hearth’s center lies an oak barrel five feet tall and two feet in diameter against the southern wall. The east wall between the hearth and the north wall runs a crystal shelf eight feet long, rising four feet above floor level and projecting one foot into the room; it holds polished crystal glasses. The north wall hosts a polished counter twelve feet along its length with a three‑foot width projecting into the tavern. Its front edge sits one foot from the northern wall. At the north‑west corner of this counter stands a high bar stool, three feet tall with a seat 1½ by 1 foot in size. West of the back door, two feet east of its center, is a raised back table five by seven feet on a plinth keeping it one foot above floor level. The polished tables—six rectangular pieces each four by six feet—are set across the central area, spaced at least three feet apart to allow passage between them and toward the counter or hearth. Each of the six tables has a lantern stand two feet tall positioned on its surface; the lanterns hold oil lamps that illuminate their immediate areas without blocking movement. The entire space is anchored by the back door and stone hearth, with all other objects fixed in place relative to these anchors.

RAW SCENE
Amy steps forward from behind the polished counter on the north wall, her rough‑spun tunic scuffs visible as she walks across the creaking wooden floor toward a barrel perched against the east side of the stone hearth.

She reaches the oak barrel in about one second, lifts a spoon from her leather apron pocket, and scoops ale into the goblin’s chipped mug held by his green skin. The liquid swirls around the mug as she turns to face the counter where the goblin leans over it.

Amy pours the ale into the mug with a steady hand, then steps back toward the north wall, pockets the spoon in her apron and positions herself behind the polished counter beside the leaning goblin.

The tavern is a rectangular room thirty feet east‑west by forty feet north‑south, with a creaking wooden floor of four‑inch planks spaced half an inch apart. The west wall hosts the back door at its center; a wooden latch attaches to a three‑foot wide frame one foot above the floor and opens eastward into the tavern. The south wall contains a stone hearth centered along the wall, twelve feet long with an eight‑foot depth. Its fire surface is six by four feet, surrounded on three sides by a two‑foot high stone surround. East of the hearth’s center lies an oak barrel five feet tall and two feet in diameter against the southern wall. The east wall between the hearth and the north wall runs a crystal shelf eight feet long, rising four feet above floor level and projecting one foot into the room; it holds polished crystal glasses. The north wall hosts a polished counter twelve feet along its length with a three‑foot width projecting into the tavern. Its front edge sits one foot from the northern wall. At the north‑west corner of this counter stands a high bar stool, three feet tall with a seat 1½ by 1 foot in size. West of the back door, two feet east of its center, is a raised back table five by seven feet on a plinth keeping it one foot above floor level. The polished tables—six rectangular pieces each four by six feet—are set across the central area, spaced at least three feet apart to allow passage between them and toward the counter or hearth. Each of the six tables has a lantern stand two feet tall positioned on its surface; the lanterns hold oil lamps that illuminate their immediate areas without blocking movement. The entire space is anchored by the back door and stone hearth, with all other objects fixed in place relative to these anchors.

RAW SCENE

Amy walks from behind the polished counter toward the back table, carrying a small pot of steaming brew on her left hand. She lifts a crystal chalice with her right hand and tilts the pot to pour the hot liquid into the chalice, steam curling up between them.

She sets the crystal chalice gently onto the wooden surface of the back table beside the elf’s chair, ensuring it rests without wobbling. Amy nods thanks toward the elf, a quiet smile crossing her face as she steps slightly away from the table.

STATIC SETTING AUTHORITY
The tavern is a rectangular room thirty feet east‑west by forty feet north‑south, with a creaking wooden floor of four‑inch planks spaced half an inch apart. The west wall hosts the back door at its center; a wooden latch attaches to a three‑foot wide frame one foot above the floor and opens eastward into the tavern. The south wall contains a stone hearth centered along the wall, twelve feet long with an eight‑foot depth. Its fire surface is six by four feet, surrounded on three sides by a two‑foot high stone surround. East of the hearth’s center lies an oak barrel five feet tall and two feet in diameter against the southern wall. The east wall between the hearth and the north wall runs a crystal shelf eight feet long, rising four feet above floor level and projecting one foot into the room; it holds polished crystal glasses. The north wall hosts a polished counter twelve feet along its length with a three‑foot width projecting into the tavern. Its front edge sits one foot from the northern wall. At the north‑west corner of this counter stands a high bar stool, three feet tall with a seat 1½ by 1 foot in size. West of the back door, two feet east of its center, is a raised back table five by seven feet on a plinth keeping it one foot above floor level. The polished tables—six rectangular pieces each four by six feet—are set across the central area, spaced at least three feet apart to allow passage between them and toward the counter or hearth. Each of the six tables has a lantern stand two feet tall positioned on its surface; the lanterns hold oil lamps that illuminate their immediate areas without blocking movement. The entire space is anchored by the back door and stone hearth, with all other objects fixed in place relative to these anchors.
